# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 25 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 1

## Sūkta 68 (Maṇḍala 8)

श्री (*śrī*)

श्री महागणाधिपतये नमः (*śrī mahāgaṇādhipataye namaḥ*)

वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे । यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमामि गजाननं ॥ (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame | yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ ‖*)

यस्य निश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*yasya niśvasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyo 'khilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*)

वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्यायी सदाशिवः । तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyī sadāśivaḥ | tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*)

Ṛgveda-Saṃhitā with Sāyaṇa's Bhāṣya, Part 25.

अथ षष्ठाष्टके पंचमोऽध्यायः (*atha ṣaṣṭhāṣṭake paṃcamo 'dhyāyaḥ*)

The fifth chapter in the sixth Aṣṭaka. The sixty-eighth sūkta in the eighth maṇḍala.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ॥ श्रीगणेशाय नमः ॥
> यस्य निःश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् ।
> निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरम् ॥
> षष्ठे चतुर्थमध्यायं श्रीमायणसुतः सुधीः ॥
> व्याख्याय सायणाचार्यः पंचमं व्याकरोत्यथ ॥
> तत्र ‘आ त्वा रथम्' इत्येकोनविंशत्यृचं नवमं सूक्तम् । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका-' आ त्वैकोना प्रियमेध आदावनुष्टुम्मुखास्तृचाश्चत्वारोऽंत्याः षळृक्षाश्वमेधयोर्दानस्तुतिः' इति । आंगिरसः प्रियमेध ऋषिः। प्रथमाचतुर्थीसप्तमीदशम्योऽनुष्टुभः शिष्टाः परं गायत्रम्' इति परिभाषया गायत्र्यः । अंततः षट्स्वृक्षु ऋक्षाश्वमेधयोर्दानं स्तूयते । अतस्तास्तद्देवताकाः । शिष्टा अनुक्तपरिभाषयैंद्र्यः । सूक्तविनियोगो लैंगिकः। अद्यस्तृचो मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपत्। तथा च सूत्रितम्--- ‘ आ त्वा रथं यथोतय इदं वसो सुतमंध इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपदनुचरौ' (आश्व. श्रौ. ५. १४) इति । महाव्रतादिष्वपि यत्र तृचांतरं न विधीयते तत्र सर्वत्रायमेव प्रतिपद्भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Salutation to Gaṇeśa. Sāyaṇa bows to Vidyātīrtha-Maheśvara, from whose breath the Vedas came and who made the whole world out of the Vedas. The wise Sāyaṇa, son of Māyaṇa, having explained the fourth chapter in the sixth Aṣṭaka, now explains the fifth. In it the ninth sūkta, आ त्वा रथम् (*ā tvā ratham*), has nineteen Ṛks. The Anukramaṇikā says of it: आ त्वैकोना प्रियमेध आदावनुष्टुम्मुखास्तृचाश्चत्वारोऽंत्याः षळृक्षाश्वमेधयोर्दानस्तुतिः (*ā tvaikonā priyamedha ādāvanuṣṭummukhāstṛcāścatvāro 'ṃtyāḥ ṣaḷṛkṣāśvamedhayordānastutiḥ*). The seer is Priyamedha of the Āṅgirasa line. By the rule that the first, fourth, seventh and tenth Ṛks are Anuṣṭubh, the rest are Gāyatrī. In the last six Ṛks the gift of Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha is praised, so these have that praise of gifts as their deity; by the rule for what is not stated, the remaining Ṛks are addressed to Indra. The use of the sūkta in ritual is by indication. Its first tṛca is the opening verse-triad of the Marutvatīya. So it is laid down in the sūtra आ त्वा रथं यथोतय इदं वसो सुतमंध इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपदनुचरौ (*ā tvā rathaṃ yathotaya idaṃ vaso sutamaṃdha iti marutvatīyasya pratipadanucarau*) (Āśv. Śrau. 5. 14) **NOTE**. In the Mahāvrata and other rites too, wherever no other tṛca is prescribed, this one is the opening tṛca.

### Printed p. 2

##### Anuvāda

In the eighth maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda-Saṃhitā the seventh anuvāka contains ten sūktas in all. Of these, eight have been explained in the previous chapter. Now this sūktā beginning आ त्वा रथं (*ā tvā rathaṃ*) is the ninth in this anuvāka. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: आ त्वैकोना प्रियमेध आदावनुष्टुप्खास्तृचाश्चत्वारोऽन्त्याः षळृक्षाश्वमेधयोर्दानस्तुतिः (*ā tvaikonā priyamedha ādāvanuṣṭupkhāstṛcāścatvāro 'ntyāḥ ṣaḷṛkṣāśvamedhayordānastutiḥ*). The seer of this sūkta is Priyamedha, a son of Aṅgiras. The first, fourth, seventh and tenth Ṛks of this sūkta are in the Anuṣṭubh metre; the remaining Ṛks are in the Gāyatrī metre. In the last six Ṛks the praise of the gifts of the two, Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha, is stated. Therefore the praise of their gifts is the deity of those Ṛks. The remaining Ṛks have Indra as deity. The use of the sūkta in ritual is by indication. The first three Ṛks of this sūkta are to be recited as the opening tṛca in the Marutvatīya śastra-mantras, as is explained in the Āśvalāyana-Śrauta-Sūtra by the sūtra आ त्वा रथं यथोतय इदं वसो सुतमंध इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपदनुचरौ (*ā tvā rathaṃ yathotaya idaṃ vaso sutamaṃdha iti marutvatīyasya pratipadanucarau*) (Ā. 5-14). And in the Mahāvrata and other rites, if there is no express rule that other mantras be recited for the opening tṛca, then this same tṛca mentioned above is to be recited as the opening tṛca.

##### Particulars

Sūkta 68.

Maṇḍala 8. Anuvāka 7. Sūkta 68.

Aṣṭaka 6. Adhyāya 5. Vargas 1, 2, 3, 4.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 19.

Seer: Priyamedha.

Deity: 1-13, Indra; 14-19, the praise of the gifts of Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha.

Metre: 1, 4, 7, 10, Anuṣṭubh; 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9, 11-19, Gāyatrī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ त्वा॒ रथं॒ यथो॒तये॑ सु॒म्नाय॑ वर्तयामसि ।  
> तु॒वि॒कू॒र्मिमृ॑ती॒षह॒मिंद्र॒ शवि॑ष्ठ॒ सत्प॑ते ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 3

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । त्वा । रथम् । यथा । ऊतये । सुम्नाय । वर्तयामसि ।  
> तुविऽकूर्मिम् । ऋतिऽसहम् । इंद्र । शविष्ठ । सत्ऽपते ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वा त्वाम् आ वर्तयामसि आवर्तयामः । किमर्थम् । ऊतये अस्माकं रक्षणाय सुम्नाय सुखाय च । किमिव । रथं यथा । रथं यथोतये सुखाय चावर्तयंति तद्वत् । कीदृशं त्वाम् । तुविकूर्मिं बहुकर्माणम् ऋतीषहं हिंसकानामभिभवितारम् । हे इंद्र शविष्ठ अतिशयेन बलवन् हे सत्पते सतां पालक त्वामिति समन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, we turn you hither (ā vartayāmasi means "we bring you here"). For what? For our protection, and for happiness. Like what? As men turn a chariot hither for protection and for happiness, so we bring you. What kind of you? One of many deeds, the subduer of those who would harm. O Indra, most mighty, O protector of the good: the construction is "you".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शविष्ठ (*śaviṣṭha*)—possessed of exceeding strength
- सत्पते (*satpate*)—protector of good men
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- तुविकूर्मिं (*tuvikūrmiṃ*)—doer of many kinds of deeds
- ऋतीषहं (*ṛtīṣahaṃ*)—one who overthrows hostile enemies
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for our protection
- सुम्नायु (*sumnāyu*)—also for our happiness
- रथं यथा (*rathaṃ yathā*)—as a chariot brings you
- आ वर्तयामसि (*ā vartayāmasi*)—we bring

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O most mighty Indra, protector of the good, you who do deeds of many kinds and overthrow hostile enemies: as a chariot brings you, so we also bring you, for our protection and for our happiness.

##### English translation
Most powerful Indra, protector of the good, we bring you here, rich in achievement and subduer of enemies, as a car for our protection and weal.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तुवि॑शुष्म॒ तुवि॑क्रतो॒ शची॑वो॒ विश्व॑या मते ।  
> आ प॑प्राथ महित्व॒ना ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 4

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तुविऽशुष्म । तुविक्रतो इति तुविऽक्रतो । शचीऽवः । विश्वया । मते ।  
> आ । पप्राथ । महिऽत्वना ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे तुविशुष्म प्रभूतबल हे तुविक्रतो बहुकर्मन् । अथवा बहुप्रज्ञ कर्मणः पृथगभिधानात् । हे शचीवः बहुकर्मोपेत मते पूजनीयेंद्र विश्वया विश्वव्याप्तेन महित्वना महत्त्वेन आ पप्राथ आपूरितवानसि । अविशेषाद्विश्वमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Tuviśuṣma, of abundant strength; O Tuvikrato, of many deeds, or else of much wisdom, since karma is stated separately; O Śacīvaḥ, endowed with many deeds; O adorable Indra: you have filled everything, with your majesty that pervades all (viśvayā). Since nothing is specified, the meaning is "the whole world".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तुविशुष्म (*tuviśuṣma*)—possessed of exceeding strength
- तुविक्रतो (*tuvikrato*)—possessed of manifold wisdom
- शचीवः (*śacīvaḥ*)—endowed with many kinds of deeds
- मते (*mate*)—and worshipful, O Indra
- विश्वया (*viśvayā*)—all
- महित्वना (*mahitvanā*)—with your greatness
- आ पप्राथ (*ā paprātha*)—you have filled your power everywhere

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, possessed of exceeding strength, of manifold wisdom, endowed with many kinds of deeds, and worshipful: with all your greatness you have filled your power everywhere.

##### English translation
Great in power, rich in deeds, mighty one, adorable, you have filled (all things) with your universal majesty.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यस्य॑ ते महि॒ना म॒हः परि॑ ज्मा॒यंत॑मी॒यतुः॑ ।  
> हस्ता॒ वज्रं॑ हिर॒ण्यय॑म् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यस्य । ते । महिना । महः । परि । ज्मायंतम् । ईयतुः ।  
> हस्ता । वज्रम् । हिरण्ययम् ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 5

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> महः महतः यस्य ते तव । यच्छब्दः प्रकृतपरामर्शकः । प्रकृतं तूक्तमृग्द्वयम् । तत्रत्यतुविकूर्मिमृतीषहमित्याद्युक्तलक्षणस्य तवेत्यर्थः । महिना महत्त्वेन हस्ता तव हस्ता हस्तौ ज्मायंतं पृथिव्यां सर्वतो व्याप्नुवंतं हिरण्ययं हिरण्मयं वज्रम् ईयतुः परिगृह्णीतः । सर्वदास्माकं भयनिवारणायेति भावः ॥
> पृष्ठ्याभिप्लवषडहयोर्द्वितीयेऽहनि ‘विश्वानरस्य' इति प्रतिपत्तृचः । सूत्रितं च- ‘ विश्वानरस्य वस्पतिमिंद्र इत्सोमपा एक इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपदनुचरौ' (आश्व. श्रौ. ७. ६ ) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Mahaḥ means "great". "Of whom": the word yasya refers to what has gone before, the two Ṛks just given; that is, of you who are of the nature stated there, "of many deeds, subduer of those who would harm" and so on. By your greatness, your two hands grasp (īyatuḥ means "take hold of") the golden thunderbolt that pervades the earth on every side (jmāyantam). The sense is: always, for the removal of our fear.

On the second day of the Pṛṣṭhya and Abhiplava ṣaḍahas the Ṛk beginning विश्वानरस्य (*viśvānarasya*) is the opening tṛca. It is laid down in the sūtra विश्वानरस्य वस्पतिमिंद्र इत्सोमपा एक इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपदनुचरौ (*viśvānarasya vaspatimiṃdra itsomapā eka iti marutvatīyasya pratipadanucarau*) (Āśv. Śrau. 7. 6) **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) यस्य (*yasya*)—possessed of the qualities mentioned before
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—and great
- ते (*te*)—your
- महिना (*mahinā*)—by greatness itself
- हस्ता (*hastā*)—your two hands
- परि ज्मायंतं (*pari jmāyaṃtaṃ*)—pervading everywhere
- हिरण्ययं (*hiraṇyayaṃ*)—made of gold
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—the thunderbolt weapon
- ईयतुः (*īyatuḥ*)—hold

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, possessed of the qualities described before, and great: by your greatness itself your two hands hold the thunderbolt weapon, which pervades everywhere and is made of gold.

##### English translation
You mighty one, whose hands in your might grasp the all-pervading golden thunderbolt.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि॒श्वान॑रस्य व॒स्पति॒मना॑नतस्य॒ शव॑सः ।  
> एवै॑श्च चर्षणी॒नामू॒ती हु॑वे॒ रथा॑नाम् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वानरस्य । वः । पतिम् । अनानतस्य । शवसः ।  
> एवैः । च । चर्षणीनाम् । ऊती । हुवे । रथानाम् ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 6

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> विश्वानरस्य विश्वान् शत्रून् प्रत्यृतस्य अनानतस्य शत्रूणामप्रह्वस्य शवसः बलस्य पतिं स्वामिनमिंद्रं वा । अत्रेंद्रसंबंधिनो मरुतोऽपि संकीर्त्यंते । हे मरुतः वः। युष्माकमित्यर्थः । यद्यपि मरुत्संशब्दनं नास्ति तथापि व इति सामर्थ्याल्लभ्यते । युष्माकं चर्षणीनां सैनिकानाम् एवैः गमनैः सह । यद्वा चर्षणीनाम् इंद्रस्य सेनारूपाणां वो युष्माकं गमनैरिति सामानाधिकरण्यम् । युष्माकं रथानां च ऊती ऊतिभिर्गमनैश्च सह हुवे आह्वयामि । गंतृभी रथैर्गंतृभिर्मरुद्भिश्च सहेंद्रं हुव इत्यर्थः । यद्वा । हे यजमानाः युष्मदीयाः सैनिकाः सरथा यदा प्रविशंति युद्धाय संग्रामं तदानीं तेषां साहाय्यायेंद्रं हुव इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The lord (pati), Indra, of the strength (śavasaḥ) that is viśvānara, directed against all enemies, and anānata, to which enemies do not bow. Here the Maruts, who belong to Indra, are also mentioned: O Maruts, vaḥ means "of you"; although the word "Marut" is not present, it is understood from vaḥ by implication. Along with the goings (evaiḥ) of you, the soldiers (carṣaṇīnām); or else, as an apposition: of you, who are Indra's army, along with your goings. And with the protections or goings (ūtī) of you and of your chariots I call (huve). The meaning is: I call Indra together with the chariots that go and the Maruts that go. Or else: O sacrificers, when your soldiers with their chariots enter the battle for war, then I call Indra for their help.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Maruts) वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- चर्षणीनां (*carṣaṇīnāṃ*)—of the form of an army
- एवैः (*evaiḥ*)—him who is joined with the goings
- रथानां (*rathānāṃ*)—of your chariots
- ऊती च (*ūtī ca*)—and who is joined with the protections, and
- विश्वानरस्य (*viśvānarasya*)—of the strength that destroys all enemy folk
- अनानतस्य (*anānatasya*)—that bows to no enemy
- शवसः (*śavasaḥ*)—to the strength
- पतिं (*patiṃ*)—the lord, Indra
- हुवे (*huve*)—I call, praying

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, I call by prayer Indra, who is joined with the goings of you in the form of an army, who is joined with the protections of your chariots, and who is the lord of the strength that destroys all enemy folk and bows to no enemy.

##### English translation
I invoke (Indra) the lord of that might which subdues all enemies and bows to none,—(I invoke him) followed by your onsets as his soldiers and (surrounded) by the protection of your chariots (O Maruts).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the second day of the sacrifices called Pṛṣṭhya and Abhiplava ṣaḍaha, the three Ṛks beginning विश्वानरस्य (*viśvānarasya*) (Ṛks 4-6 of this sūkta) are, at the time of reciting the Marutvatīya Śastra-mantras,

### Printed p. 7

recited as the opening tṛca, as is explained in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra by the sūtra विश्वानरस्य वनस्पतिमिंद्र इत्योमुपा एक इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रदिपदनुचरौ (*viśvānarasya vanaspatimiṃdra ityomupā eka iti marutvatīyasya pradipadanucarau*) (Ā. 7-6). **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भिष्ट॑ये स॒दावृ॑धं॒ स्व॑र्मीळ्हेषु॒ यं नरः॑ ।  
> नाना॒ हवं॑त ऊ॒तये॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभिष्टये । सदाऽवृधम् । स्वःऽमीळ्हेषु । यम् । नरः ।  
> नाना । हवंते । ऊतये ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे यजमानाः युष्माकम् अभिष्टये साहाय्यार्थमभिगमनायाभिष्टये वा सदावृधं सर्वदा वर्धयंतं सेवकान् स्वयं सर्वदा वर्धमानं वा हुव इति शेषः । यं स्वर्मीळ्हेषु संग्रामेषु नरः नेतारो मनुष्याः नाना बहुप्रकारं हवंते आह्वयंति ऊतये रक्षार्थं तं हुव इति शेषः ॥ ॥ १ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O sacrificers, for your help, that is, for coming to your aid (abhiṣṭaye), or for the attainment of what is desired: I call (this verb is to be supplied) him who always increases (sadāvṛdham) his servants, or who himself always grows. I call him whom men, the leaders, in many ways (nānā) call upon in battles (svarmīḷheṣu) for protection (ūtaye).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O sacrificers) यं (*yaṃ*)—whom, which Indra
- स्वर्निर्ह्लेषु (*svarnirhleṣu*)—in battles
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—men
- नाना (*nānā*)—in many ways
- हवंते (*havaṃte*)—call; such a one
- सदावृधं (*sadāvṛdhaṃ*)—Indra, who always makes his devotees grow, or who always grows himself
- अभिष्टये (*abhiṣṭaye*)—for the attainment of what is desired
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—and for protection (let us call, praying)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificers, let us call, with prayer, for the attainment of what is desired and for protection, that Indra whom men in many ways call upon in battles, who always makes his devotees grow or who himself always grows.

### Printed p. 8

##### English translation
(I invoke him) to come to our help, whose might ever waxes more and more,—to whom men appeal for aid in various ways in battles.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प॒रोमा॑त्र॒मृची॑षम॒मिंद्र॑मु॒ग्रं सु॒राध॑सम् ।  
> ईशा॑नं चि॒द्वसू॑नाम् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> परःऽमात्रम् । ऋचीषमम् । इंद्रम् । उग्रम् । सुऽराधसम् ।  
> ईशानम् । चित् । वसूनाम् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> परोमात्रम् । परा मात्रा यस्य तादृशम् । अथवा मीयत इति मात्रं दूरदेशः । ततः परस्ताद्वर्तमानमपरिमितस्वरूपम् । तथापि ऋचीषमम् ऋचा स्तुत्या समम् । यद्यप्यपरिच्छिन्नः तथापि स्तुतिर्यावन्मात्रं विषयीकरोति तत्सम इत्यर्थः । तदेवाह। इंद्रं परमैश्वर्ययोगादिच्छानुकुलस्वरूपम् उग्रम् उद्गूर्णबलं सुराधसम् । राध इति धननाम । शोभनधनं शोभनान्नं वा ईशानं चित् ईश्वरं च । केषाम् । वसूनाम् अस्मभ्यं प्रदेयानां गवादिधनानाम् । एवंमहानुभावमिंद्रं हुव इति शेषः ॥
> तृतीयेऽहनि मरुत्वतीये ‘तंतमित्' इति प्रतिपत्तृचः । सूत्रितं च- ‘ तंतमिद्राधसे महे त्रय इंद्रस्य सोमाः' (आश्व. श्रौ. ७. १०) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Paromātram means one whose measure (mātrā) is beyond; or else mātra is that which is measured, a distant region, and he is beyond that, of unmeasured nature. Even so he is ṛcīṣama, equal to the ṛc, the praise: though unbounded, he is equal to whatever extent the praise takes him as its object. Of that very Indra, through supreme lordship, the nature accords with his wish; he is ugra, of uplifted strength; surādhas, since rādhas is a name for wealth: of fine wealth, or of fine food; and also lord (īśānam cit) of what? Of the riches (vasūnām), cows and the like, that are to be given to us. Such an exalted Indra I call (this verb is to be supplied).

On the third day, in the Marutvatīya, the opening tṛca is the one beginning तंतमित् (*taṃtamit*). It is laid down in the sūtra तंतमिद्राधसे महे त्रय इंद्रस्य सोमाः (*taṃtamidrādhase mahe traya iṃdrasya somāḥ*) (Āśv. Śrau. 7. 10) **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- परोमात्रं (*paromātraṃ*)—one who exceeds measure
- ऋचीषमं (*ṛcīṣamaṃ*)—one who is worthy of the best praise
- उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)—one of exceeding strength
- सुराधसं (*surādhasaṃ*)—one possessing the best wealth
- ईशानं चित् (*īśānaṃ cit*)—and lord of the whole universe
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*)—for the sake of wealth (let us call, praying)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Let us call with prayer, for the sake of wealth, Indra, who exceeds measure, is worthy of the best praise, is of exceeding strength, possesses the best wealth, and is lord of the whole universe.

### Printed p. 9

##### English translation
(I invoke) Indra, the unlimited, worthy of praise, the mighty, possessing excellent wealth, the lord of treasures (for his votaries).

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तंत॒मिद्राध॑से म॒ह इंद्रं॑ चोदामि पी॒तये॑ ।  
> यः पू॒र्व्यामनु॑ष्टुति॒मीशे॑ कृष्टी॒नां नृ॒तुः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तम्ऽतम् । इत् । राधसे । महे । इंद्रम् । चोदामि । पीतये ।  
> यः । पूर्व्याम् । अनुऽस्तुतिम् । ईशे । कृष्टीनाम् । नृतुः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तंतमित् तमेव इंद्रम् । सर्वेष्वपि यागकालेषु तमेवेंद्रमित्यर्थः। तं प्रति चोदामि प्रेरयामि स्तुतिं पीतये सोमपानाय । ततः को लाभ इति उच्यते । महे महते राधसे धनाय प्रभूतधनलाभार्थम् । यः नृतुः फलस्य नेता देवः पूर्व्यां पूर्वे भवां यज्ञमुखस्थाम् अनुष्टुतिम् अनुक्रमेण क्रियमाणां स्तुतिं कृष्टीनां मनुष्याणामृत्विजां संबंधिनीम् ईशे ईष्टे श्रोतुं तं चोदामीति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Tamtamit means that very Indra, at all the times of the sacrifice. To him I direct (codāmi, "I urge") my praise, for the drinking of soma (pītaye). What is the gain from it? It is said: for great wealth (mahe rādhase), that is, to obtain abundant riches. The god who is nṛtu, the leader of the fruit, is able (īśe, that is, īṣṭe) to hear the earlier (pūrvyām), that is, the first, that stands at the mouth of the sacrifice, praise (anuṣṭutim) made in due order, belonging to men, the priests (kṛṣṭīnām). The connection is: him I urge.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नृतुः (*nṛtuḥ*)—the bringer of the fruit
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- कृष्टीनां (*kṛṣṭīnāṃ*)—made by priests and others
- पूर्व्यं (*pūrvyaṃ*)—the principal one in the sacrifice
- अनुष्टुतिं (*anuṣṭutiṃ*)—and recited in order, the praise
- ईशे (*īśe*)—is able to hear
- तं तमित् इंद्रं (*taṃ tamit iṃdraṃ*)—that same Indra
- महे (*mahe*)—great
- राधसे (*rādhase*)—(to me) for the gain of wealth
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—to drink the soma
- चोदामि (*codāmi*)—I urge

### Printed p. 10

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

That Indra, the bringer of the fruit, who is able to hear the praise made by priests and others, principal in the sacrifice and recited in due order, that same Indra I urge to drink the soma, so that great wealth may come to me.

##### English translation
To him, to him, Indra, I direct my praise, that he may quaff the Soma to my great gain,—to him, the bringer of success, who rules over the praises of the offerers at the opening of the sacrifice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the third day of the sacrifice, at the time of reciting the Marutvatīya Śastra mantras, the three Ṛks beginning तंतमित् (*taṃtamit*) (Ṛks 7-9 of this sūkta) are to be recited as the opening tṛca, as is explained in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra by the sūtra तंतमिद्राधसे महे त्रय इंद्रस्य सोमाः (*taṃtamidrādhase mahe traya iṃdrasya somāḥ*) (Ā. 7-10).

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न यस्य॑ ते शवसान स॒ख्यमा॒नंश॒ मर्त्यः॑ ।  
> नकिः॒ शवां॑सि ते नशत् ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । यस्य । ते । शवसान । सख्यम् । आनंश । मर्त्यः ।  
> नकिः । शवांसि । ते । नशत् ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शवसान बलवन्निंद्र यस्य ते तव सख्यं मर्त्यः मरणधर्मा मनुष्यः न आनंश न व्याप्नोति ते शवांसि बलान्यपि नकिः नैव नशत् व्याप्नोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O mighty Indra (śavasāna), a mortal, a man subject to death, does not attain (ānaṃśa, "does not pervade") the friendship of you. Your strengths too no one attains (nakiḥ naśat, "none at all reaches").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शवसान (*śavasāna*)—O Indra, possessed of power
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of what great qualities
- ते (*te*)—your

### Printed p. 11

- सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—friendship
- मर्त्यः (*martyaḥ*)—no man
- न आनंश (*na ānaṃśa*)—can obtain
- ते (*te*)—of such a you
- शवांसि (*śavāṃsi*)—the powers also
- नकिः नशत् (*nakiḥ naśat*)—no one can reach

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, possessed of power, no man can obtain your friendship, possessed of such great qualities, and no one can reach even your powers.

##### English translation
You mighty one, whose friendship no mortal reaches, whose might no one attains.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वोता॑स॒स्त्वा यु॒जाप्सु सूर्ये॑ म॒हद्धन॑म् ।  
> जये॑म पृ॒त्सु व॑ज्रिवः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वाऽऊतासः । त्वा । युजा । अप्ऽसु । सूर्ये । महत् । धनम् ।  
> जयेम । पृत्ऽसु । वज्रिऽवः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वोतासः त्वया रक्षिताः त्वा त्वया युजा सहायेन अप्सु स्नातुं सूर्यं द्रष्टुं च । स्नानादिव्यवहारं कर्तुं सूर्ये उदिते सति गमनादिव्यवहारं कर्तुमित्यर्थः। तदर्थ पृत्सु संग्रामेषु हे वज्रिवः वज्रवन्निंद्र महद्धनं जयेम । शत्रून् संग्रामे जित्वा तेषां धनं लभेमेत्यर्थः ॥
> चतुर्थेऽहनि मरुत्वतीये ‘तं त्वा यज्ञेभिः' इति तृचः प्रतिपत् । सूत्रितं च -- तं त्वा यज्ञेभिरीमह इदं वसो सुतमंध इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपदनुचरौ' ( आश्व. श्रौ. ७. ११) इति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, protected by you (tvotāsaḥ), with you as our ally (yujā): to bathe in the waters (apsu) and to behold the sun (sūrye); that is, when the sun has risen, to carry on bathing and other activities, and going about and the like. For that purpose, in battles (pṛtsu), O wielder of the thunderbolt (vajrivaḥ), Indra, may we win great wealth (mahaddhanam); that is, having conquered our enemies in battle, may we obtain their wealth.

On the fourth day, in the Marutvatīya, the tṛca beginning तं त्वा यज्ञेभिः (*taṃ tvā yajñebhiḥ*) is the opening one. It is laid down in the sūtra तं त्वा यज्ञेभिरीमह इदं वसो सुतमंध इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपदनुचरौ (*taṃ tvā yajñebhirīmaha idaṃ vaso sutamaṃdha iti marutvatīyasya pratipadanucarau*) (Āśv. Śrau. 7. 11) **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वज्रिवः (*vajrivaḥ*)—O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt weapon
- त्वोतासः (*tvotāsaḥ*)—protected by you
- त्वा युजा (*tvā yujā*)—we who have obtained your help
- अप्सु (*apsu*)—to bathe in the sacred waters
- सूर्ये (*sūrye*)—to behold the sun when it has risen
- पृत्सु (*pṛtsu*)—in battles
- महद्धनं (*mahaddhanaṃ*)—exceeding wealth
- जयेम (*jayema*)—may we obtain

### Printed p. 12

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt weapon, may we, protected by you and having obtained your help, obtain exceeding wealth in battles, so that we may bathe in the sacred waters and behold the sun when it has risen.

##### English translation
Protected by you, O thunderer, with you as our ally, may we win great wealth in battles, that we may bathe in the water and behold the sun.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तं त्वा॑ य॒ज्ञेभि॑रीमहे॒ तं गी॒र्भिर्गि॑र्वणस्तम ।  
> इंद्र॒ यथा॑ चि॒दावि॑थ॒ वाजे॑षु पुरु॒माय्य॑म् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तम् । त्वा । यज्ञेभिः । ईमहे । तम् । गीःऽभिः । गिर्वणःऽतम ।  
> इंद्र । यथा । चित् । आविथ । वाजेषु । पुरुऽमाय्यम् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तं स्तुत्यत्वेन प्रसिद्धं त्वा त्वां यज्ञेभिः यागसाधनैः सोमादिभिः ईमहे याचामहे । तम् एवेंद्रं गीर्भिः स्तुतिभिरीमहे । हे गिर्वणस्तम गीर्भिः स्तुतिभिर्वननीयतम इंद्र तं त्वामिति समन्वयः । हे इंद्र त्वं यथा चिदाविथ येन प्रकारेण ररक्षिथ माम् । चिदिति पूरणः । कुत्रेति उच्यते । वाजेषु संग्रामेषु । कीदृशं माम् । पुरुमाय्यं बहुप्रज्ञम् । बहुस्तुतिमित्यर्थः ॥ ॥ २ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

To you (tvā), famous as the one to be praised, we make our request (īmahe, "we ask") with the means of sacrifice, soma and the like (yajñebhiḥ). To that same Indra we make request with praises (gīrbhiḥ). O Girvaṇastama, most to be loved with praises, O Indra: the construction is "to you". O Indra, as (yathā) you protected (āvitha) me, in what way you protected me; cit is an expletive. Where? In battles (vājeṣu). What kind of me? Puru-māyya, of much wisdom, that is, much praised.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गिर्वणस्तम (*girvaṇastama*)—worshipped with praises
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यथा चित् (*yathā cit*)—in what manner
- पुरुमाय्यं (*purumāyyaṃ*)—me, who have much wisdom
- वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*)—in battles
- आविथ (*āvitha*)—

### Printed p. 13

protected, (in the same way)

- तं (*taṃ*)—the famous
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- यज्ञेभिः (*yajñebhiḥ*)—with the offering of soma, the means of sacrifice
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we worship and beseech
- तं (*taṃ*)—that same you
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with praises also we extol and beseech

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, worshipped with praises: in the same way in which you protected me, who have abundant wisdom, in battle, we worship and beseech you, the famous, with the offering of soma, the means of sacrifice. With praises also we extol and beseech that same you.

##### English translation
We address you with sacrificial gifts, (we address) you with songs, O Indra, most worthy of song, as you have protected me, the offerer of many praises, in battles

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the fourth day of the sacrifice, at the time of reciting the Marutvatīya Śastra mantras, the three Ṛks beginning तं त्वा यज्ञेभिः (*taṃ tvā yajñebhiḥ*) (Ṛks 10-12 of this sūkta) are to be recited as the pratipat-sūcana, as is explained in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra by the sūtra तं त्वा यज्ञेभिरीमह इदं वसो सुतमंध इति मरुत्वतीयस्य प्रतिपदनुचरौ (*taṃ tvā yajñebhirīmaha idaṃ vaso sutamaṃdha iti marutvatīyasya pratipadanucarau*) (Ā. 7-11). **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यस्य॑ ते स्वा॒दु स॒ख्यं स्वा॒द्वी प्रणी॑तिरद्रिवः ।  
> य॒ज्ञो वि॑तंत॒साय्यः॑ ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यस्य । ते । स्वादु । सख्यम् । स्वाद्वी । प्रऽनीतिः । अद्रिऽवः ।  
> यज्ञः । वितंतसाय्यः ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अद्रिवः वज्रवन्निंद्र यस्य स्तुत्यत्वेन प्रसिद्धस्य ते तव सख्यं स्वादु अतीवानुभवार्हम् । किंच ते प्रणीतिः प्रणयनं धनादीनां स्वाद्वी स्वादु सुहर्षकम् । तथोभे त्वद्विषयो यज्ञः च वितंतसाय्यः विशेषेण तननीयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Adrivaḥ, wielder of the thunderbolt, Indra: your friendship (sakhyam), you who are famed as the one to be praised, is sweet, exceedingly fit to be enjoyed. Moreover your praṇīti, the leading forward of wealth and the like, is sweet (svādvī), well delighting. And likewise both (the sacrifice) directed to you and the sacrifice is vitantasāyya, to be extended in a special manner. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 14

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*)—O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt weapon
- यस्य (*yasya*)—who are exceedingly famous
- ते (*te*)—with you
- सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—friendship
- स्वादु (*svādu*)—is exceedingly beneficial
- प्रणीतिः (*praṇītiḥ*)—the gifts of wealth and the like that proceed from you also
- स्वाद्वी (*svādvī*)—are delight-giving
- यज्ञः (*yajñaḥ*)—the sacrifice directed to you also
- वितंतसाय्यः (*vitaṃtasāyyaḥ*)—are performed at length

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt weapon, friendship with you, who are exceedingly famous, is exceedingly beneficial. The gifts of wealth and the like that proceed from you also are delight-giving. The sacrifice directed to you also is performed at length.

##### English translation
You, the thunderer, whose friendship is sweet, sweet too is your liberality, and your sacrifice pre-eminently to be performed.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒रु ण॑स्त॒न्वे॒३॒॑ तन॑ उ॒रु क्षया॑य नस्कृधि ।  
> उ॒रु णो॑ यंधि जी॒वसे॑ ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उरु । नः । तन्वे । तने । उरु । क्षयाय । नः । कृधि ।  
> उरु । नः । यंधि । जीवसे ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं नः अस्माकं तन्वे आत्मजाय उरु प्रभूतं कृधि कुरु । सामर्थ्याद्धनं सुखं वेति गम्यते । तथा तने तत्पुत्राय उरु कृधि । तथा क्षयाय निवासाय उरु कृधि । नः अस्माकं जीवसे जीवनाय यंधि प्रयच्छाभिमतम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, make ample (uru, "abundant") for our tanū, our own offspring; by implication it is understood that wealth or happiness is meant. Likewise make ample for tana, his son. Likewise make ample for kṣaya, dwelling. Grant (yaṃdhi means "give") what we wish for our living (jīvase).

### Printed p. 15

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) नः (*naḥ*)—our
- तन्वे (*tanve*)—for sons and the like
- उरु (*uru*)—abundant wealth
- कृधि (*kṛdhi*)—give
- तने (*tane*)—for their sons and the like also
- उरु (*uru*)—give abundant wealth
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- क्षयाय (*kṣayāya*)—for dwelling
- उरु (*uru*)—build ample dwelling places
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- जीवसे (*jīvase*)—for living
- यंधि (*yaṃdhi*)—provide, in abundance, what we wish

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, give abundant wealth to our sons and the like. Give abundant wealth to their sons and the like also. For our dwelling build ample dwelling places. For our living provide, in abundance, what we wish.

##### English translation
Give ample (wealth) to our own selves, give ample (wealth) to our children, give ample (wealth) to our dwelling,—grant us (our desire) that we may live.

#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒रुं नृभ्य॑ उ॒रुं गव॑ उ॒रुं रथा॑य॒ पंथा॑म् ।  
> दे॒ववी॑तिं मनामहे ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उरुम् । नृऽभ्यः । उरुम् । गवे । उरुम् । रथाय । पंथाम् ।  
> देवऽवीतिम् । मनामहे ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र नृभ्यः अस्मदीयेभ्यो भृत्येभ्यः उरुं हितं मनामहे याचामहे । तथा गवे । एतदुपलक्षणम् । गवाश्वादिकाय तथा रथाय पंथां पंथानं मार्गम् । अथवा नृप्रभृतीनां संचाराय शोभनं मार्गं मनामहे । तथा देववीतिं यज्ञं मनामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, for our servants (nṛbhyaḥ) we ask (manāmahe, "we beseech") what is ample (uru), that is, beneficial. Likewise for the cow (gave); this is only an illustration: for cattle, horses and the like. Likewise for the chariot we ask a path (panthām), a way; or else, for the movement of men and the like we ask a good road. Likewise we ask for devavīti, the sacrifice.

### Printed p. 16

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) नृभ्यः (*nṛbhyaḥ*)—for our servants and other people
- उरुं (*uruṃ*)—abundant welfare
- मनामहे (*manāmahe*)—we ask
- गवे (*gave*)—for our cattle and the like
- उरुं (*uruṃ*)—wide
- पंथां (*paṃthāṃ*)—we ask for a road
- रथाय (*rathāya*)—for the movement of our chariots
- उरुं (*uruṃ*)—we ask for a wide road
- देववीतिं (*devavītiṃ*)—we ask also for the conveniences of the sacrifices offered to the gods

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, for our servants and other people we ask abundant welfare. For our cattle and the like we ask a wide road. For the movement of our chariots also we ask a wide road. We ask also for the conveniences of the sacrifices offered to the gods.

##### English translation
We solicit a spacious (road) for our servants, a spacious road for our cattle, a spacious road for our chariot, and (an abundant) sacrifice.

#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उप॑ मा॒ षड्द्वाद्वा॒ नरः॒ सोम॑स्य॒ हर्ष्या॑ ।  
> तिष्ठं॑ति स्वादुरा॒तयः॑ ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उप । मा । षट् । द्वाऽद्वा । नरः । सोमस्य । हर्ष्या ।  
> तिष्ठंति । स्वादुऽरातयः ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एतदाद्याः षड़ृच ऋक्षाश्वमेधयोर्दानस्तुतिरूपाः । यद्यपि बृहद्देवतानुक्रमण्याम् ‘ऋक्षाश्वमेधयोरत्र पंच दानप्रशंसकाः' (बृहद्दे. ६. ९२ ) इत्युक्तं तथापि उप मा षट्' इत्यस्या राजदानस्तुतिशेषत्वादविरोधः । अनेनैवाशयेनानुक्रमण्याम् ' अंत्याः षळृच ऋक्षाश्वमेधयोर्दानस्तुतिः' इत्युक्तम् । मा मां प्रियमेधं यज्ञे प्रसर्पंतः षट् एतत्संख्याकाः नरः नेतारो राजानः सोमस्य पीतस्य हर्ष्या हर्षेण स्वादुरातयः सुष्ठूपभोगार्हदानाः संतः द्वाद्वा द्वौ द्वौ पितृपुत्ररूपेण युग्मौ भूत्वा माम् उप तिष्ठंति । तेषां युग्मानां नाम तूत्तरत्र स्पष्टीक्रियते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Ṛk and the five after it, six Ṛks in all, are in the form of praise of the gifts of Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha. Although in the Bṛhaddevatā-Anukramaṇī it is said ऋक्षाश्वमेधयोरत्र पंच दानप्रशंसकाः (*ṛkṣāśvamedhayoratra paṃca dānapraśaṃsakāḥ*) (Bṛhadde. 6. 92) **NOTE**, there is no contradiction, because the Ṛk उप मा षट् (*upa mā ṣaṭ*) is a remainder of the praise of a king's gift. With this very intention the Anukramaṇikā says अंत्याः षळृच ऋक्षाश्वमेधयोर्दानस्तुतिः (*aṃtyāḥ ṣaḷṛca ṛkṣāśvamedhayordānastutiḥ*). Mā, "me", Priyamedha: six in number (ṣaṭ), leaders (nara), kings who advance at the sacrifice, with the delight (harṣyā) of the soma drunk, their gifts fit to be well enjoyed (svādurātayaḥ), becoming pairs (dvādvā), two and two, as father and son, stand by me (upa tiṣṭhanti). The names of those pairs are made plain further on.

### Printed p. 17

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- द्वाद्वा (*dvādvā*)—who are in groups of two and two, in the form of father and son
- स्वादुरातयः (*svādurātayaḥ*)—who have gifts that bring welfare
- षट् (*ṣaṭ*)—who are six in number
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—the kings
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—produced by the drinking of the soma juice
- हर्ष्या (*harṣyā*)—with joy
- मा (*mā*)—me
- उप तिष्ठंति (*upa tiṣṭhaṃti*)—approach and stand

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The kings, who are in groups of two and two in the form of father and son, who have gifts that bring welfare and who are six in number, approach me and stand by me, filled with the joy produced by the drinking of the soma juice.

##### English translation
Six princes come to me in pairs, bearing pleasant gifts, in the exhilaration of the Soma.

#### Ṛk 15

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒ज्राविं॑द्रो॒त आ द॑दे॒ हरी॒ ऋक्ष॑स्य सू॒नवि॑ ।  
> आ॒श्व॒मे॒धस्य॒ रोहि॑ता ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋज्रौ । इंद्रोते । आ । ददे । हरी इति । ऋक्षस्य । सूनवि ।  
> आश्वऽमेधस्य । रोहिता ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रोते एतन्नामक आतिथिग्वेऽतिथिग्वनाम्नो राज्ञः पुत्रे । ' अतिथिग्वाय शंबरम्' (ऋ. सं. १. ५१. ६)  ‘ अतिथिग्वाय शंस्यम्' (ऋ. सं. ६. २६. ३) इत्यादिष्वतिथिग्वः प्रसिद्धः । तत्पुत्र इंद्रोते ऋज़ौ ऋजुगामिनावश्वौ आ ददे स्वीकृतवानस्मि । तथा ऋक्षस्य सूनवि ऋक्षनाम्नः पुत्रेऽन्यस्मिन् राजनि हरी हरितवर्णावश्वौ आ ददे। तथा आश्वमेधस्य अश्वमेधपुत्रे राजनि रोहिता रोहितवर्णावश्वौ आ ददे। नन्वनुक्रमण्यामुभयोरेव दानप्रशंसारूपत्वमुक्तम् अत्र कथं त्रयाणां दानकीर्तनमिति । नैष दोषः । ऋक्षाश्वमेधपुत्रयोरेव यागेऽस्यर्षेः प्रवृत्तेस्तयोरेव दानं प्रस्तुतम्। इंद्रोतस्तु स्वपित्रा सह तयोर्यज्ञदिदृक्षयागत्य तयोर्दानं दृष्ट्वा स्वपित्रा प्रेरितो दत्तवानश्वौ। अतस्तद्दानं प्रासंगिकमिति । एवमृक्षाश्वमेधयोर्दानस्तुतित्वं न व्याहन्यते । पितृपुत्रयोरभेदात्तयोः पक्षकर्तृत्वाच्च। इंद्रोतदानस्थ प्रासंगिकत्वं ‘षळश्वान्' इत्यत्र विस्पष्टयिष्यते ॥
> [ xxv. 2 ]

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indrote is one so named, the son of the king named Atithigva. Atithigva is well known in such passages as अतिथिग्वाय शंबरम् (*atithigvāya śaṃbaram*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 51. 6) and अतिथिग्वाय शंस्यम् (*atithigvāya śaṃsyam*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6. 26. 3). From his son Indrote I have received (ā dade, "I have accepted") the two horses that go straight (ṛjrau). Likewise from the son of Ṛkṣa (ṛkṣasya sūnavi), another king, I have received the two green horses (harī). Likewise from the son of Aśvamedha, a king, I have received the two red horses (rohitā).

An objection: in the Anukramaṇikā the character of praise of gifts is stated of the two only; how then is the giving of three celebrated here? This is no fault. The seer's activity at the sacrifice was only with the sons of Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha, so the gift of those two is the subject. But Indrote, having come with his own father out of a wish to see the sacrifice of those two, and seeing their gift, was prompted by his father and gave the horses. So his gift is incidental. In this way the character of praise of the gifts of Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha is not contradicted, because father and son are not different, and because those two are the doers of the side. **NOTE** The incidental character of Indrote's gift will be made plain at षळश्वान् (*ṣaḷaśvān*).

### Printed p. 18

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रोते (*iṃdrote*)—from Indrota, the son of Atithigva
- ऋज्रौ (*ṛjrau*)—two horses that move straight
- आ ददे (*ā dade*)—I accepted
- ऋक्षस्य (*ṛkṣasya*)—of one named Ṛkṣa
- सूनवि (*sūnavi*)—from the son
- हरी (*harī*)—I obtained two green-coloured horses
- आश्वमेधस्य (*āśvamedhasya*)—from the king, the son of Aśvamedha
- रोहिता (*rohitā*)—I obtained also two gold-coloured horses

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

From Indrota, the son of Atithigva, I accepted two horses that move straight. From the son of one named Ṛkṣa I obtained two green-coloured horses. From the king, the son of Aśvamedha, I obtained two gold-coloured horses.

##### English translation
I receive two straight-going steeds from Indrota, two bay from the son of Riksha, two roan from the son of Ashwamedha.

#### Ṛk 16

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒रथां॑ आतिथि॒ग्वे स्व॑भी॒शूंरा॒र्क्षे ।  
> आ॒श्व॒मे॒धे सु॒पेश॑सः ॥ १६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सुऽरथान् । आतिथिऽग्वे । सुऽअभीशून् । आर्क्षे ।  
> आश्वऽमेधे । सुऽपेशसः ॥ १६ ॥

### Printed p. 19

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आतिथिग्वे इंद्रोते सुरथान् शोभनरथोपेतानश्वानाददे । आर्क्षे ऋक्षपुत्रे स्वभीशून् अश्वानाददे । आश्वमेधे अश्वमेधपुत्रे 'सुपेशसः सुरूपानश्वान् शोभनालंकारानाददे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

From Atithigva's son Indrota I have received (ādade) horses furnished with excellent chariots. From the son of Ṛkṣa I have received horses with excellent reins. From the son of Aśvamedha I have received horses of beautiful form, finely adorned.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आतिथिग्वे (*ātithigve*)—from Indrota, the son of Atithigva
- सुरथान् (*surathān*)—I obtained horses furnished with excellent chariots
- आर्क्षे (*ārkṣe*)—from the son of Ṛkṣa
- स्वभीशून् (*svabhīśūn*)—I obtained horses possessed of excellent bridles
- आश्वमेधे (*āśvamedhe*)—from the son of Aśvamedha
- सुपेशसः (*supeśasaḥ*)—I obtained horses possessed of excellent form

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

From Indrota, the son of Atithigva, I obtained horses furnished with excellent chariots. From the son of Ṛkṣa I obtained horses possessed of excellent bridles. From the son of Aśvamedha I obtained horses possessed of excellent form.

##### English translation
(I receive) two steeds with excellent chariots from the son of Atithigva, two with excellent reins from the son of Riksha, two with excellent ornaments from the son of Ashwamedha.

#### Ṛk 17

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> षळश्वाँ॑ आतिथि॒ग्व इं॑द्रो॒ते व॒धूम॑तः ।  
> सचा॑ पू॒तक्र॑तौ सनम् ॥ १७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> षट् । अश्वान् । आतिथिऽग्वे । इंद्रोते । वधूऽमतः ।  
> सचा । पूतऽक्रतौ । सनम् ॥ १७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आतिथिग्व इंद्रोते पूतक्रतौ शुद्धप्रज्ञे शुद्धकर्मोपेते वा तस्मिन् वधूमतः वधूभिर्वडवाभिस्तद्वतः षडश्वान् सचा ऋक्षाश्वमेधयोः पुत्राभ्यां दत्तेनाश्वादिधनेन सचा सह सनं लब्धवानस्मि । एतत्साहित्यवचनमिंद्रोतदानस्य प्रासंगिकत्वे लिंगम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In Indrota, the son of Atithigva, who is pure in mind or pure in action, with his mares (vadhūmataḥ), I have obtained six horses, together (sacā) with the horses and other wealth given by the two sons of Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha. This mention of the two together is the indication (liṅga) that the gift of Indrota is incidental.

### Printed p. 20

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आतिथिग्वे (*ātithigve*)—the son of Atithigva
- इंद्रोते (*iṃdrote*)—from Indrota
- पूतक्रतौ (*pūtakratau*)—possessed of pure action
- वधूमतः (*vadhūmataḥ*)—joined with mares
- षट् अश्वान् (*ṣaṭ aśvān*)—six horses
- सचा (*sacā*)—together with the other wealth given by the sons of Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha
- सनं (*sanaṃ*)—I have obtained

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

From Indrota, the son of Atithigva, I have obtained six horses, pure in action and joined with mares, together with the other wealth given by the sons of Ṛkṣa and Aśvamedha.

##### English translation
I have received together (with my other gifts) six horses with their mares from the pious Indrota, the son of Atithigva.

#### Ṛk 18

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऐषु॑ चेत॒द्वृष॑ण्वत्यं॒तरृ॒ज्रेष्वरु॑षी ।  
> स्व॒भी॒शुः कशा॑वती ॥ १८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । एषु । चेतत् । वृषण्ऽवती । अंतः । ऋज्रेषु । अरुषी ।  
> सुऽअभीशुः । कशाऽवती ॥ १८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एषु ऋज्रेषु ऋजुगामिष्वश्वेषु अंतः मध्ये आ चेतत् आज्ञायते । का। वृषण्वती वर्षकैः पुमश्वैस्तद्वती अरुषी आरोचमाना स्वभीशुः शोभनप्रग्रहा कशावती दृप्ता वडवा ज्ञायते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Among these straight-going horses (ṛjreṣu), a mare is known (cetat): she is accompanied by bulls, that is, stallions that shed seed; she is shining, with fine reins, with a whip, spirited.

### Printed p. 21

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋज्रेषु (*ṛjreṣu*)—of straight movements
- एषु (*eṣu*)—of these horses
- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—in the midst
- वृषण्वती (*vṛṣaṇvatī*)—accompanied by a stallion capable of impregnating
- अरुषी (*aruṣī*)—of an attractive golden colour
- स्वभीशुः (*svabhīśuḥ*)—possessed of an excellent bridle
- कशावती (*kaśāvatī*)—possessed of a whip; one excellent mare is known to be there
- आ चेतत् (*ā cetat*)—is known

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the midst of these horses of straight movements, one excellent mare is known: accompanied by a stallion capable of impregnating, of an attractive golden colour, possessed of an excellent bridle, possessed of a whip.

##### English translation
Among these straight-going steeds is numbered a mature roan mare with excellent reins and whip.

#### Ṛk 19

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न यु॒ष्मे वा॑जबंधवो निनि॒त्सुश्च॒न मर्त्यः॑ ।  
> अ॒व॒द्यमधि॑ दीधरत् ॥ १९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । युष्मे इति । वाजऽबंधवः । निनित्सुः । चन । मर्त्यः ।  
> अवद्यम् । अधि । दीधरत् ॥ १९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वाजबंधवः अन्नबंधवोऽन्नप्रदाः । एवं पुत्राणां पितृपुत्ररूपाणां षण्णां वा संबोधनम् । हे राजानः युष्मे युष्मासु निनित्सुश्चन निंदकोऽपि मर्त्यः मनुष्यः अवद्यं निंदा न अधि दीधरत् नान्वधारयत्। नारोपयति युष्मासु । अतोऽनिंद्या यूयमिति दातॄणां स्तुतिः ॥ ॥ ४ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O food-kinsmen (vājabandhavaḥ), that is, givers of food: this is an address to the sons, the father and sons, or the six. O kings, even a mortal who is a censurer (ninitsuḥ) has laid no censure (avadyam), no blame, upon you; he does not place it on you. Therefore you are beyond blame: thus the givers are praised.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वाजबंधवः (*vājabaṃdhavaḥ*)—O kings all, who are lords of the wealth of food and give food away

### Printed p. 22

- निनित्सुः (*ninitsuḥ*)—whose very nature is to censure always
- मर्त्यः चन (*martyaḥ cana*)—even a man
- युष्मे (*yuṣme*)—in regard to you
- अवद्यं (*avadyaṃ*)—a word of censure
- न अधि दीधरत् (*na adhi dīdharat*)—does not utter at all

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O kings all, who are lords of the wealth of food and give food away, even a man whose very nature is to censure always utters no word of censure in regard to you. Your conduct is so praiseworthy.

##### English translation
O princes, givers of food, even the lover of calumny has thrown no censure on you.

## Sūkta 69 (Maṇḍala 8)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘प्रप्र वः' इत्यष्टादशर्चं दशमं सूक्तं प्रियमेधस्यांगिरसस्यार्षम् । द्वितीया ‘नदं वः' इत्येषा चतुःसप्तकोष्णिक् चतुर्थ्याद्यास्तिस्रो गायत्र्य एकादशीषोडश्यौ पंक्ती शिष्टा दशानुष्टुभः ।‘अपादिंद्रः इत्यर्धर्चो वैश्वदेवो ‘वरुण इदिह' इत्याद्यास्त्रयोऽर्धर्चा वरुणदेवताकाः शिष्टा ऐंद्र्यः । तथा चानुक्रांतं—‘प्रप्र द्व्यूनानुष्टुभं द्विबृहत्यंतं द्वितीयोष्णिक् चतुर्थ्याद्यास्तिस्रो गायत्र्यः षोळश्येकादश्यौ पंक्ती अपाद्वैश्वदेवोऽर्धर्चस्त्रयो वारुणाः' इति । आद्यस्तृचः षोडशिशस्त्र आनुष्टुभः । सूत्रितं 'च- ‘ प्रप्र वस्त्रिष्टुभमिषमर्चत प्रार्चत ' (आश्व. श्रौ. ६. २) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The tenth sūkta, of eighteen Ṛks, beginning प्रप्र वः (*prapra vaḥ*), has Priyamedha, the son of Aṅgiras, as seer. The second Ṛk, नदं वः (*nadaṃ vaḥ*), is in the Uṣṇih metre of four lines of seven syllables (चतुःसप्तकोष्णिक् (*catuḥsaptakoṣṇik*)); the three Ṛks from the fourth are Gāyatrī; the eleventh and sixteenth are Paṅkti; the remaining ten are Anuṣṭubh. The half-Ṛk अपादिंद्रः (*apādiṃdraḥ*) has the Viśvedevas as deity; the three half-Ṛks from वरुण इदिह (*varuṇa idiha*) have Varuṇa; the rest have Indra. The Anukramaṇikā states the same. The first three Ṛks are Anuṣṭubh Ṛks for the Ṣoḍaśin śastra, as laid down in the sūtra प्रप्र वस्त्रिष्टुभमिषमर्चत प्रार्चत (*prapra vastriṣṭubhamiṣamarcata prārcata*) (Āśvalāyana Śrauta-Sūtra 6. 2).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning प्रप्र वः (*prapra vaḥ*) is the tenth sūkta in the seventh anuvāka. It has eighteen Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Priyamedha, a son of the seer Aṅgiras. In this sūkta the second Ṛk, नदं वः (*nadaṃ vaḥ*), is in the metre called चतुःसप्तकोष्णिक् (*catuḥsaptakoṣṇik*). This Ṛk has four lines, each line having seven syllables: such is its characteristic. The three Ṛks after the fourth Ṛk (Ṛks 4-6) are in the Gāyatrī metre. The eleventh and sixteenth Ṛks are in the Paṅkti metre. The remaining Ṛks are in the Anuṣṭubh metre. The first half of the eleventh Ṛk, अपादिंद्रः (*apādiṃdraḥ*), has the Viśvedevas as deity; the second half of this Ṛk, वरुण इदिह (*varuṇa idiha*), and both halves of the next Ṛk (the twelfth) have Varuṇa as deity.

### Printed p. 23

The remaining Ṛks have Indra as deity. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: प्रप्र द्व्यृनाऽनुष्टुभं द्विबृहत्यंतं द्वितीयोष्णिक् चतुर्थाद्यास्तिस्रो गायत्र्यः षोळश्येकादश्यौ पंक्ती अपाद्वैश्वदेवोऽर्धर्चस्त्रयो वारुणाः (*prapra dvyṛnā 'nuṣṭubhaṃ dvibṛhatyaṃtaṃ dvitīyoṣṇik caturthādyāstisro gāyatryaḥ ṣoḷaśyekādaśyau paṃktī apādvaiśvadevo 'rdharcastrayo vāruṇāḥ*). The first three Ṛks of this sūkta are used for the Anuṣṭubh Ṛks among the mantras of the Ṣoḍaśin śastra, as is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana-Śrauta-Sūtra, प्रप्र वस्त्रिष्टुभमिषमर्चत प्रार्चते (*prapra vastriṣṭubhamiṣamarcata prārcate*) (Ā. 6-2).

##### Particulars

Sūkta 69.

Maṇḍala 8. Anuvāka 7. Sūkta 69.

Aṣṭaka 6. Adhyāya 5. Vargas 5, 6, 7.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 18.

Seer: Priyamedha.

Deity: 1-10, 13-18, Indra; 11, first half, the Viśvedevas; 11, second half, 12, Varuṇa.

Metre: 1-3 **NOTE**, 7-10, 12-15 **NOTE**, Anuṣṭubh; 2, Uṣṇih; 4-6, Gāyatrī; 11, 16, Paṅkti; 17, 18, Bṛhatī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रप्र॑ वस्त्रि॒ष्टुभ॒मिषं॑ मं॒दद्वी॑रा॒येंद॑वे ।  
> धि॒या वो॑ मे॒धसा॑तये॒ पुरं॒ध्या वि॑वासति ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रऽप्र । वः । त्रिऽस्तुभम् । इषम् । मंदत्ऽवीराय । इंदवे ।  
> धिया । वः । मेधऽसातये । पुरम्ऽध्या । आ । विवासति ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अध्वर्य्वादयः वः यूयम् । प्रथमार्थे द्वितीया । त्रिष्टुभं स्तोभत्रयोपेतम् इषम् अन्नं प्रप्र । अपरः प्रशब्दः पूरणः । प्रभरतेति शेषः । उपसर्गश्रुतेर्योग्यक्रियाध्याहारः । कस्मै । मंदद्वीराय । यो वीरान हर्षयति स मंदद्वीरः । तस्मै इंदवे इंद्राय । इंदतेरैश्वर्यकर्मणः इदं रूपम् । अथवा फलैर्वृष्टिभिर्वोनत्तीतींदुरिंद्रः । तस्मै । स चेंद्रः वः युष्मान मेधसातये यज्ञसंभजनाय पुरंध्या बहुप्रज्ञया धिया कर्मणा आ विवासति । अभिमतफलयोजनेन सत्करोतीत्यर्थः ॥
> महाव्रते निष्केवल्ये ‘नदं वः' इत्येषा विहरणीया । तथैव पंचमारण्यके सूत्रितं च -- नदं व ओदतीनामित्येतयैतानि व्यतिषजति ' ( ऐ. आ. ५. १. ६ ) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Adhvaryus and the rest, you (vaḥ, the accusative put for the nominative) bring forward (the second प्र (*pra*) is a filler; "bring forward" is to be supplied, a suitable verb being understood because a preverb is heard) the food (iṣam) of three stomas, to Indu, that is, Indra, who gladdens heroes; the form comes from the root इंद (*iṃda*) in the sense of lordship; or Indu is he who wets with fruits and rains. That Indra honours you, that is, favours you by granting the desired fruit, with a work (dhiyā) of much wisdom, for the carrying out of the sacrifice. In the Mahāvrata, at the Niṣkevalya, the Ṛk नदं वः (*nadaṃ vaḥ*) is to be interspersed; so it is laid down in the fifth Āraṇyaka (Ai. Ā. 5. 1. 6).

### Printed p. 24

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O priests) वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- मंदद्वीराय (*maṃdadvīrāya*)—who bring joy to heroic men
- इंदवे (*iṃdave*)—addressing Indra, the lord
- त्रिष्टुभं (*triṣṭubhaṃ*)—possessed of three kinds of praise
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- प्रप्र (*prapra*)—supply
- सः (*saḥ*)—that same Indra
- वः (*vaḥ*)—to you
- मेधसातये (*medhasātaye*)—so that it may be favourable for the conduct of the sacrifice
- पुरंध्या (*puraṃdhyā*)—possessed of many kinds of wisdom
- धिया (*dhiyā*)—with works
- आ विवासति (*ā vivāsati*)—favours

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, addressing Indra, the lord, who brings joy to heroic men, supply food possessed of three kinds of praise. That same Indra favours you with works possessed of many kinds of wisdom, so that it may be favourable for the conduct of the sacrifice.

##### English translation
Present your sacrificial food with a three-fold song of praise to Indu, gladdener of heroes; he will bless you in your religious rites to the accomplishment of your sacrifice.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न॒दं व॒ ओद॑तीनां न॒दं योयु॑वतीनाम् ।  
> पतिं॑ वो॒ अघ्न्या॑नां धेनू॒नामि॑षुध्यसि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नदम् । वः । ओदतीनाम् । नदम् । योयुवतीनाम् ।  
> पतिम् । वः । अघ्न्यानाम् । धेनूनाम् । इषुध्यसि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ओदतीनाम् । ओदत्य उषसः  ‘ ओदती भास्वती' इति तन्नामसु पाठात् । तासां नदम् । उत्पादकमित्यर्थः । इंद्रेण ह्युषस उत्पद्यंत इंद्रस्यैव सूर्यत्वात् । ' विवस्वदिंद्र सुग्म्य' इति द्वादशादित्यमध्य इंद्रः पठितः । तादृशमिंद्रं हे यजमानाः वः युष्मदर्थम् । आह्वयतेत्यर्थः । तथा योयुवतीनां सर्वत्र मिश्रयंतीनां नदीनां नदं' शब्दयितारं वः युष्मदर्थमाह्वयामि । अघ्न्यानाम् अहंतव्यानां गवां पतिम् आह्वये । अथ प्रत्यक्षकृता । हे यजमान त्वं धेनूनां क्षीरादिना प्रीणयित्रीणां गवाम् इषुध्यसि अन्नमिच्छसि ॥
> अग्निहोत्रे पूर्वाहुतौ हुतायां ‘ता अस्य' इत्यनयोत्तरामाहुतिं कांक्षमाणस्तिष्ठेत् । तथा च सूत्रितं -- ता अस्य सूददोहस इति पूर्वामाहुतिमुपोत्थायोत्तरां कांक्षेत ' ( आश्व. श्रौ. २. ३ ) इति । महाव्रते निष्केवल्येऽप्येषा । तथैव पंचमारण्यके सूत्रितं- ता अस्य सूददोहस इत्येतदादिः सूददोहाः सूददोहाः ' ( ऐ. आ. ५. १. ६ ) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Odatīs are the Dawns, since the Nighaṇṭu reads ओदती भास्वती (*odatī bhāsvatī*) among their names. Their nada, that is, their producer: for the Dawns are born from Indra, because Indra is the sun; Indra is read among the twelve Ādityas in विवस्वदिंद्र सुग्म्य (*vivasvadiṃdra sugmya*). O sacrificers, invoke such an Indra for yourselves. Likewise I call for you the sounder (nada) of the rivers that mingle everywhere (yoyuvatīs), and the lord of the unslayable cows (aghnyās). Then, addressing the sacrificer directly: O sacrificer, you desire the food of the milch cows, who gladden with milk and the like. In the Agnihotra, when the first oblation has been offered, he should stand, awaiting the next oblation, with this Ṛk, ता अस्य (*tā asya*); so it is laid down in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-Sūtra 2. 3. This Ṛk is also used in the Mahāvrata at the Niṣkevalya; so it is laid down in the fifth Āraṇyaka (Ai. Ā. 5. 1. 6).

### Printed p. 25

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ओदतीनां (*odatīnāṃ*)—(O sacrificers) of all the dawns
- नदं (*nadaṃ*)—Indra, their producer
- वः (*vaḥ*)—for your purpose, invoke him with prayer
- योयुवतीनां (*yoyuvatīnāṃ*)—of the rivers that flow everywhere
- नदं (*nadaṃ*)—Indra, the maker of the roaring sound
- वः (*vaḥ*)—invoke him for your purpose
- अघ्न्यानां (*aghnyānāṃ*)—of all the cows that are not fit to be slain
- पतिं (*patiṃ*)—Indra, the lord; I shall invoke him for you (O sacrificer)
- धेनूनां (*dhenūnāṃ*)—of the milch cows that satisfy with milk and the like
- इषुध्यसि (*iṣudhyasi*)—you desire the milk and the food that they provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificers, invoke with prayer, for your purpose, Indra, the producer of all the dawns. Invoke, for your purpose, Indra, the maker of the roaring sound, as the rivers that flow everywhere roar. I shall invoke for you Indra, the lord of all the cows that are not fit to be slain. O sacrificer, you desire the milk and the food that the milch cows, who satisfy with milk and the like, provide.

##### English translation
(Invoke) for yourselves the author of the dawns, (I invoke) for you the roarer of the rivers ; (I invoke) for you the lord of the inviolable ones ; (O sacrificer), you desire cows.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the Mahāvrata sacrifice, when the Niṣkevalya Śastra mantras are recited, this Ṛk, नदं वः (*nadaṃ vaḥ*), is to be recited as a Viharaṇīya Ṛk; this is stated in the Fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence नदं व ओदतीना-मित्येतयैतानि व्यतिषजति (*nadaṃ va odatīnā-mityetayaitāni vyatiṣajati*) (Ai. Ā. 5-1-6).

### Printed p. 26

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता अ॑स्य॒ सूद॑दोहसः॒ सोमं॑ श्रीणंति॒ पृश्न॑यः ।  
> जन्मं॑दे॒वानां॒ विश॑स्त्रि॒ष्वा रो॑च॒ने दि॒वः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ताः । अस्य । सूदऽदोहसः । सोमम् । श्रीणंति । पृश्नयः ।  
> जन्मन् । देवानाम् । विशः । त्रिषु । आ । रोचने । दिवः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ताः प्रसिद्धाः सूददोहसः । सूद इति कूपनाम । तत्सदृशदोहनाः पृश्नयः पृश्निवर्णा गावः अस्य इंद्रस्य सोमं श्रीणंति मिश्रयंत्याशिरेण । कदा । त्रिषु त्रिष्वपि सवनेषु । गावो विशेष्यंते । देवानां जन्मन् जन्मस्थाने । दिवीत्यर्थः । दिवः आदित्यस्य आ रोचने आरोचमाने । अनेन द्यौर्विशेष्यते । तस्मिन् स्थाने विशः निविशंत्यः । यज्ञार्थोपयुक्तानां गवां द्युप्राप्तिः प्रसिद्धा ॥
> द्वितीये पर्याये मैत्रावरुणशस्त्रे ‘ अभि प्र गोपतिम् ' इति तृचोऽनुरूपः ।. सूत्रितं च-- ‘ अभि त्वा वृषभा सुतेऽभि प्र गोपतिं गिरा ' ( आश्व. श्रौ. ६. ४ ) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those well-known dappled cows, whose milking is like that of a well (Sāyaṇa explains that the word for "well" is the name of a well), mix the Soma of this Indra with the milk-mixture, at all three pressings. The cows are further described: they abide in the birthplace of the gods, that is, in heaven, in the shining place of the sun; by "shining" the sky is qualified, and in that place the cows settle. It is well known that cows used in the sacrifice reach heaven.

In the second round, in the Śastra of the Maitrāvaruṇa priest, the triplet beginning अभि प्र गोपतिम् (*abhi pra gopatim*) is the Anurūpa; it is so laid down in the sūtra अभि त्वा वृषभा सुतेऽभि प्र गोपतिं गिरा (*abhi tvā vṛṣabhā sute 'bhi pra gopatiṃ girā*) (Āśva. Śrau. 6. 4).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods
- जन्मन् (*janman*)—in the atmosphere, the place of birth
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the sun
- आ रोचने (*ā rocane*)—in the shining heaven
- विशः (*viśaḥ*)—those that dwell
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—the well-known ones
- सूददोहसः (*sūdadohasaḥ*)—that give milk abundantly, like the water in a deep well
- पृश्नयः (*pṛśnayaḥ*)—cows of many kinds of colour
- अस्य (*asya*)—for this Indra
- त्रिषु (*triṣu*)—at all three times of the sacrifice
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma
- श्रीणंति (*śrīṇaṃti*)—they mix with the milk-mixtures

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The cows that dwell in the shining heaven of the sun, which is in the atmosphere, the birthplace of the gods, the well-known ones, that give milk abundantly like the water in a deep well, and are of many kinds of colour,

### Printed p. 27

mix the Soma for this Indra with the milk-mixtures at all three times of the sacrifice.

##### English translation
These white cows, giving milk like wells, mix the Soma for him at the three oblations, rising (in consequence to the brilliant home of the sun, the birthplace of the gods).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the Agnihotra rite, after the first oblation has been offered, one rises and stands, awaiting the offering of the second oblation, and recites this Ṛk, ता अस्य (*tā asya*); this is explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-Sūtra ता अस्य सूददोहस इति पूर्वामाहुतिमुपोत्थायोत्तरां कांक्षेत (*tā asya sūdadohasa iti pūrvāmāhutimupotthāyottarāṃ kāṃkṣeta*) (Ā. 2-3). In the Mahāvrata sacrifice also this Ṛk is to be recited among the Niṣkevalya Śastra mantras; it is stated in the Fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence ता अस्य सूददोहस इत्येतदादिः सूददोहाः सूददोहाः (*tā asya sūdadohasa ityetadādiḥ sūdadohāḥ sūdadohāḥ*) (Ai. Ā. 5-1-6).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भि प्र गोप॑तिं गि॒रेंद्र॑मर्च॒ यथा॑ वि॒दे ।  
> सू॒नुं स॒त्यस्य॒ सत्प॑तिम् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभि । प्र । गोऽपतिम् । गिरा । इंद्रम् । अर्च । यथा । विदे ।  
> सूनुम् । सत्यस्य । सत्ऽपतिम् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> गोपतिं गवां स्वामिनम् इंद्रम् अभि प्र अर्च । प्रकर्षेण पूजय स्तुत्या । यथा विदे  स यथा स्वात्मानं स्तुतप्रकारं जानाति यथा वा यागं प्रति गंतव्यमिति जानाति तथार्चेति । कीदृशमिंद्रम् । सत्यस्य यज्ञस्य सत्यस्य वा सूनुं पुत्रम् । तत्रानुरक्तत्वात् सूनुरित्युपचर्यते । सत्पतिं सतां यजमानानां पालकम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Worship, with praise, Indra, the lord of cows; worship him intensely. "As he knows" means: worship in the manner in which he knows himself and the manner of the praise, or in the manner in which he knows that he must go to the sacrifice. What sort of Indra? He is the son of truth, that is, of the sacrifice or of truth; he is called "son" figuratively because of his devotion to it. He is the lord of the good, that is, the protector of the sacrificers.

### Printed p. 28

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गोपतिं (*gopatiṃ*)—(O sacrificer) Indra, the lord of cows
- सत्यस्य (*satyasya*)—of the sacrifice, which is truth in its nature
- सूनुं (*sūnuṃ*)—who is as a son
- सत्पतिं (*satpatiṃ*)—the protector of the good
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- विदे (*vide*)—in the way in which he knows
- गिरा (*girā*)—with words of praise
- अभि प्र अर्च (*abhi pra arca*)—worship and praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificer, praise and worship Indra, the lord of cows, the son of the sacrifice that is truth in its nature, the protector of the good, in the way in which he knows your worship.

##### English translation
Worship with your praise, as he himself knows,—that lord of cows, Indra, the son ot truth, the protector of the good.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the second round, at the time of reciting the Śastra mantras of the Maitrāvaruṇa priest, the three Ṛks beginning अभि प्र गोपतिं (*abhi pra gopatiṃ*) are to be recited as the Anurūpa triplet; this is explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-Sūtra अभि त्वा वृषभा सुतेऽभि प्र गोपतिं गिरा (*abhi tvā vṛṣabhā sute 'bhi pra gopatiṃ girā*) (Ā. 6-4).

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ हर॑यः ससृज्रि॒रेऽरु॑षी॒रधि॑ ब॒र्हिषि॑ ।  
> यत्रा॒भि सं॒नवा॑महे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । हरयः । ससृज्रिरे । अरुषीः । अधि । बर्हिषि ।  
> यत्र । अभि । सम्ऽनवामहे ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 29

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हरयः हरितवर्णा अश्वाः अरुषीः आरोचमानाः अधि बर्हिषि । अधीति सप्तम्यर्थानुवादी । बर्हिष्यास्तृते आ ससृज्रिरे आसृजंतु । यत्र यस्मिन् बर्हिषि स्थितमिंद्रम् अभि संनवामहे अभिसंस्तुमः ॥ ॥ ५ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The bay horses, that is, those of green colour, shining, upon the sacred grass (the word "upon" only restates the sense of the locative case), let them bring down and settle there; "let them drop" is the sense. On that grass, where Indra is seated, we praise him on every side.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत्र (*yatra*)—on whatever seat of sacred grass Indra is seated, whom we shall praise and worship
- अभि संनवामहे (*abhi saṃnavāmahe*)—we shall praise and worship
- अधि बर्हिषि (*adhi barhiṣi*)—on such seats of sacred grass
- अरुषीः (*aruṣīḥ*)—the shining ones
- हरयः (*harayaḥ*)—the horses of Indra
- आ ससृज्रिरे (*ā sasṛjrire*)—let them bring Indra and seat him

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

On whatever seat of sacred grass Indra is seated, whom we praise and worship, on such seats of sacred grass let the shining horses of Indra bring Indra and seat him.

##### English translation
Let the brilliant bay (horses) drop him down on the cut grass, where we will hymn his praise.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रा॑य॒ गाव॑ आ॒शिरं॑ दुदु॒ह्रे व॒ज्रिणे॒ मधु॑ ।  
> यत्सी॑मुपह्व॒रे वि॒दत् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्राय । गावः । आऽशिरम् । दुदुह्रे । वज्रिणे । मधु ।  
> यत् । सीम् । उपऽह्वरे । विदत् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्राय गावः आशिरम् आश्रयणसाधनं पयआदिकं मधु मदकरं दुदुहे दुहते । कीदृशाय । वज्रिणे वज्रयुक्तायेंद्राय । यत् यदा उपह्वरे समीपे वर्तमानं मधु सोमरसं सीं सर्वतः विदत् लभते तदा ॥
> षोडशिशस्त्रस्य 'उद्यद्ब्रध्नस्य' इत्येषांत्या। सूत्रितं च -- उद्यद्ब्रध्नस्य विष्टपमित्येषा परिधानीया' (आश्व. श्रौ. ६. २) इति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The cows milk for Indra the milk and the like, the means of mixing, the intoxicating draught. **NOTE** What sort of Indra? The one armed with the thunderbolt. This happens when, near at hand, he finds on all sides the sweet Soma juice.

In the Śastra of the Ṣoḍaśin, the Ṛk beginning उद्यद्ब्रध्नस्य (*udyadbradhnasya*) is the last; it is so laid down in the sūtra उद्यद्ब्रध्नस्य विष्टपमित्येषा परिधानीया (*udyadbradhnasya viṣṭapamityeṣā paridhānīyā*) (Āśva. Śrau. 6. 2).

### Printed p. 30

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- उपह्वरे (*upahvare*)—the Soma juice that is near
- सीं (*sīṃ*)—everywhere
- विदत् (*vidat*)—when Indra obtains it
- वज्रिणे (*vajriṇe*)—for the bearer of the thunderbolt
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra
- गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—the cows
- मधु (*madhu*)—satisfying
- आशिरं (*āśiraṃ*)—the substance of the milk and the like, the means of mixing
- दुदुह्रे (*duduhre*)—they milk

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When Indra obtains everywhere the Soma juice that is near, then the cows milk for Indra, the bearer of the thunderbolt, the satisfying substance of the milk and the like.

##### English translation
The cows have milked the intoxicating draught for Indra, the thunderer, when he finds it near him.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उद्यद्ब्र॒ध्नस्य॑ वि॒ष्टपं॑ गृ॒हमिंद्र॑श्च॒ गन्व॑हि ।  
> मध्वः॑ पी॒त्वा स॑चेवहि॒ त्रिः स॒प्त सख्युः॑ प॒दे ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत् । यत् । ब्रध्नस्य । विष्टपम् । गृहम् । इंद्रः । च । गन्वहि ।  
> मध्वः । पीत्वा । सचेवहि । त्रिः । सप्त । सख्युः । पदे ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत् यदा ब्रध्नस्य विष्टपं सूर्यस्य स्थानं गृहमिंद्रश्च अहं चोभौ उत् गन्वहि उद्गच्छावः तदानीं मध्वः मधुरं सोमरसं पीत्वा सचेवहि संसृष्टौ भवेव । कुत्र । सख्युः सर्वेषां सखिभूतस्यादित्यस्य । त्रिः सप्त इत्यनेन देवलोकानामुत्तममेकविंशं स्थानमुच्यते आदित्यस्यैकविंशत्वात् । तथा च ब्राह्मणं- द्वादश मासाः पंचर्तवस्त्रय इमे लोका असावादित्य एकविंशः ' (ऐ. ब्रा. १. ३०) इति । तादृश एकविंशस्थाने सचेवहीति ॥
> पूर्वोक्त एव शस्त्रे  ‘ अर्चत' इति द्वितीय आनुष्टुभस्तृचः । सूत्रितं च -- ‘ प्रप्र वस्त्रिष्टुभमिषमर्चत प्रार्चत ' ( आश्व. श्रौ. ६. २) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When Indra and I, both of us, go up to the dwelling-place of the sun, then, having drunk the sweet Soma juice, we shall be united. Where? In the place of the friend of all, the sun. By "thrice seven" is meant the highest, the twenty-first place among the worlds of the gods, since the sun is the twenty-first. So the Brāhmaṇa says: द्वादश मासाः पंचर्तवस्त्रय इमे लोका असावादित्य एकविंशः (*dvādaśa māsāḥ paṃcartavastraya ime lokā asāvāditya ekaviṃśaḥ*) (Ai. Brā. 1. 30). In such a twenty-first place we shall be united.

In the same Śastra mentioned above, the second Ṛk of the Anuṣṭubh triplet is the one beginning अर्चत (*arcata*). It is so laid down in the sūtra प्रप्र वस्त्रिष्टुभमिषमर्चत प्रार्चत (*prapra vastriṣṭubhamiṣamarcata prārcata*) (Āśva. Śrau. 6. 2).

### Printed p. 31

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- ब्रध्नस्य (*bradhnasya*)—of the sun
- विष्टपं (*viṣṭapaṃ*)—the place
- गृहं (*gṛhaṃ*)—the atmosphere
- इंप्रश्च (*iṃpraśca*)—Indra and I, both together
- उत् गस्वहि (*ut gasvahi*)—when we shall climb up and reach it, then
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—the sweet Soma juice
- पीत्वा (*pītvā*)—having drunk
- सख्याः (*sakhyāḥ*)—of the sun, the friend of the whole world
- त्रिः सप्त (*triḥ sapta*)—the twenty-first, which is the highest **NOTE**
- पदे (*pade*)—in the place
- सचेवहि (*sacevahi*)—we shall be united and joined together

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When Indra and I both climb up to the atmosphere, the place of the sun, and reach it, then, having drunk the sweet Soma juice, we shall be united and joined together in the twenty-first place, the highest, of the sun, the friend of the whole world.

##### English translation
When Indra and I ascend to our home, the world of the sun, then, having drunk the sweet (Soma), let us be united in the twenty-first sphere of the (universal) friend.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the recitation of the Śastra mantras of the Ṣoḍaśin **NOTE**, this Ṛk, उद्यद्ब्रध्नस्य (*udyadbradhnasya*), is to be recited as the last Ṛk; this is explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-Sūtra उद्यद्ब्रध्नस्य विष्टपमित्येषा परिधानीया (*udyadbradhnasya viṣṭapamityeṣā paridhānīyā*) (Ā. 6–2).

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अर्च॑त॒ प्रार्च॑त॒ प्रिय॑मेधासो॒ अर्च॑त ।  
> अर्चं॑तु पुत्र॒का उ॒त पुरं॒ न धृ॒ष्ण्व॑र्चत ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अर्चत । प्र । अर्चत । प्रियऽमेधासः । अर्चत ।  
> अर्चंतु । पुत्रकाः । उत । पुरम् । न । धृष्णु । अर्चत ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 32

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अध्वर्य्वादयः यूयमिंद्रम् अर्चत पूजयत स्तुत्या। प्रार्चत प्रकर्षेणार्चतेंद्रमेव । हे प्रियमेधासः प्रियमेधसंबंधिनस्तद्गोत्रा यूयम् अर्चत इंद्रम् । पुत्रकाः पुत्रा अपि अर्चंतु इंद्रम् । उत अपि च पुरं न धृष्णु यथा पुरं धर्षणशीलमर्चंति तादृशमिंद्रम् अर्चत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Adhvaryu and the other priests, worship Indra with praise; worship him intensely, Indra alone. O Priyamedhas, that is, you who are of Priyamedha's family and lineage, worship Indra. Let the sons also worship Indra. And worship such an Indra as people worship a city that is given to assailing.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अर्चत (*arcata*)—(O priests) worship Indra
- प्रार्चत (*prārcata*)—worship him in the best manner
- प्रियमेधासः (*priyamedhāsaḥ*)—O sages, all of you of the line of Priyamedha
- अर्चत (*arcata*)—worship Indra
- पुत्रकाः (*putrakāḥ*)—your sons also
- अर्चंतु (*arcaṃtu*)—let them worship Indra
- उत (*uta*)—and
- पुरं न (*puraṃ na*)—like a city
- धृष्णु (*dhṛṣṇu*)—Indra, firm of body
- अर्चत (*arcata*)—worship him

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, worship Indra. Worship him in the best manner. O sages of the line of Priyamedha, worship Indra. Let your sons also worship Indra. And worship Indra, who is firm of body like a city.

##### English translation
Worship Indra, worship him pre-eminently, worship him, you of the family of Priyamedha ; let your sons also wosiship him ; worship him as a strong city.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the Śastra mantras mentioned before, the three Ṛks beginning अर्चतॆ (*arcatè*) are to be recited as the second triplet in the Anuṣṭubh metre; this is explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-Sūtra प्रप्र वस्त्रिष्टुभमिषमर्चत प्रार्चतॆ (*prapra vastriṣṭubhamiṣamarcata prārcatè*) (Ā. 6–2).

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अव॑ स्वराति॒ गर्ग॑रो गो॒धा परि॑ सनिष्वणत् ।  
> पिंगा॒ परि॑ चनिष्कद॒दिंद्रा॑य॒ ब्रह्मोद्य॑तम् ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 33

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अव । स्वराति । गर्गरः । गोधा । परि । सनिस्वनत् ।  
> पिंगा । परि । चनिस्कदत् । इंद्राय । ब्रह्म । उत्ऽयतम् ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> गर्गरः गर्गरध्वनियुक्तो वाद्यविशेषो युद्धे अव स्वराति भयं शब्दयति । गोधा हस्तघ्नः परि परितः सनिष्वणत् स्वनति भृशम् । पिंगा पिंगवर्णा ज्यापि परि चनिष्कदत् परिस्कंदते । यस्मादेवं युद्धः संनद्धोऽत इंद्राय ब्रह्म परिवृढं कर्म स्तुतिलक्षणम् उद्यतं भवत्विति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Gargara, a particular musical instrument with a rattling sound, sounds fear in battle. The Godhā, the hand-guard, twangs loudly all round. The tawny-coloured bowstring also leaps about on every side. Since the battle is thus joined, let a hymn, a work consisting of praise and pervading, be raised to Indra; the words "let it be" are to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गर्गरः (*gargaraḥ*)—the drum that makes the Gargara sound
- अव स्वराति (*ava svarāti*)—makes a fearful sound on the field of battle
- गोधा (*godhā*)—the drum called Godhā
- परि (*pari*)—all round
- सनिस्वणत् (*sanisvaṇat*)—sounds loudly
- पिंगा (*piṃgā*)—the golden-coloured bowstring also
- परि (*pari*)—all round
- परि चनिष्कदत् (*pari caniṣkadat*)—makes the twang
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—addressed to Indra
- ब्रह्म (*brahma*)—the excellent hymn
- उद्यतं (*udyataṃ*)—let it be recited

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The drum that makes the Gargara sound makes a fearful sound on the field of battle. The drum called Godhā sounds loudly all round. The golden-coloured string of the bow also makes the twang all round. At such a time of battle, let the excellent hymn addressed to Indra be recited.

##### English translation
The drum utters its sound, the leathern guard twangs, the tawny bowstring leaps to and fro ; let the hymn be raised to Indra.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ यत्पतं॑त्ये॒न्यः॑ सु॒दुघा॒ अन॑पस्फुरः ।  
> अ॒प॒स्फुरं॑ गृभायत॒ सोम॒मिंद्रा॑य॒ पात॑वे ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

### Printed p. 34

> आ । यत् । पतंति । एन्यः । सुऽदुघाः । अनपऽस्फुरः ।  
> अपऽस्फुरम् । गृभायत । सोमम् । इंद्राय । पातवे ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत् यदा एन्यः एतवर्णाः शुभ्रवर्णा नद्यः आ पतंति आगच्छंति सर्वतः प्रवहंति । कीदृश्यः। सुदुघाः सुदोहाः अनपस्फुरः। अपस्फुरोऽपस्फुरा अप्रवृद्धाः। अतादृश्योऽनपस्फुरः । अत्यंतं प्रवृद्धा इत्यर्थः । यद्वा । सुदुघाः प्रवृद्धा एतवर्णा गावो यदा पयआद्यर्थाय आ पतंति तदा अपस्फुरम् । अत्रापशब्दो धात्वर्थानुवादी । अत्यंतप्रवृद्धं सोममिंद्राय पातवे पातुं गृभायत ग्रहणं कुरुत । अथवानपस्फुर इत्यत्राप्यपशब्दोऽनुवादी धात्वर्थस्य । तथा सत्ययमर्थः । यदा एन्यो नद्योऽनपस्फुरोऽप्रवृद्धोदका पतंति वृष्टिरल्पा यदा भवति तदा सोममिंद्रार्थं संपादयतेति ॥ ॥६॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When the bright-coloured rivers come, that is, flow in all directions, of what kind are they? They are easy to milk and not small. "Apasphura" would mean those not grown, so the opposite word means those greatly grown. Or else: when the well-grown cows of bright colour come for milk and the like, then take the Soma, greatly grown, for Indra to drink; here the word "apa" only restates the sense of the root. Or else the word "apasphura" in the phrase also only restates the sense of the root, and the meaning is this: when the rivers come with their waters not swollen, that is, when the rain is little, then prepare the Soma for Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- एन्यः (*enyaḥ*)—those of bright colour
- सुदुघाः (*sudughāḥ*)—those to be milked well
- अनपस्फुरे (*anapasphure*)—and those that have grown very much, the cows (or the rivers)
- आ पतंति (*ā pataṃti*)—come towards us, then
- अप (*apa*)—in great abundance
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra
- पातवे (*pātave*)—to drink
- गृभायत (*gṛbhāyata*)—lift up and hold

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When the cows (or the rivers) that are of bright colour, to be milked well and greatly grown come towards us, then lift up and hold the Soma in great abundance for Indra to drink.

##### English translation
When the bright fertilizing rivers flow with diminished waters, then take the overflowing Soma for Indra to drink. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 35

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अपा॒दिंद्रो॒ अपा॑द॒ग्निर्विश्वे॑ दे॒वा अ॑मत्सत ।  
> वरु॑ण॒ इदि॒ह क्ष॑य॒त्तमापो॑ अ॒भ्य॑नूषत व॒त्सं सं॒शिश्व॑रीरिव ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अपात् । इंद्रः । अपात् । अग्निः । विश्वे । देवाः । अमत्सत ।  
> वरुणः । इत् । इह । क्षयत् । तम् । आपः । अभि । अनूषत । वत्सम् । संशिश्वरीःऽइव ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रः अपात् अपिबत् सोमम् । अग्निः अप्यपिबत् । विश्वे देवाः अपि अमत्सत तृप्ता अभवन् सोमपानेन । वरुण इत् वरुणोऽपि इह अस्मिन् यागगृहे क्षयत् निवसतु सोमपानार्थम् । निवसंतं तम् आपः अपि अभ्यनूषत । उदकान्यापनशीला स्तुतयो वाभ्यष्टुवन् । किमिव । वत्सं स्वीयं संशिश्वरीः संशिश्वर्यः संगच्छमाना गावः इव । ता यथा धावंत्यो हंभारवं कुर्वंति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra drank the Soma; Agni also drank it. All the gods also were gladdened, became satisfied by the drinking of the Soma. Let Varuṇa also dwell here in this house of the sacrifice, for the drinking of the Soma. The waters, or praises that pervade, also hailed him as he dwelt. Like what? Like cows running together to their own calf, who as they run make the sound of lowing.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- अपात् (*apāt*)—has drunk the Soma
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni also has drunk
- विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*)—the Viśvedevas
- अमुत्सत (*amutsata*)—have been gladdened
- वरुण इत् (*varuṇa it*)—Varuṇa also
- इह (*iha*)—in our house of sacrifice
- क्षयत् (*kṣayat*)—let him dwell
- वत्सं (*vatsaṃ*)—to the calf
- संशिश्वरीः इव (*saṃśiśvarīḥ iva*)—as a cow that runs to it with affection makes a sound
- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—the waters
- तं (*taṃ*)—that Varuṇa
- अभ्यनूषत (*abhyanūṣata*)—praise loudly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra has drunk the Soma. Agni also has drunk. The Viśvedevas are gladdened. Let Varuṇa also dwell in our house of sacrifice. As a cow that runs to its calf with affection makes a sound, the waters praise that Varuṇa loudly.

### Printed p. 36

##### English translation
Indra drank (the Soma), Agni drank it, the Vishwe Devah were gladdened ; let Varuna fix his dwelling here ; the waters have praised him as cows (low) meeting their calves.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒दे॒वो अ॑सि वरुण॒ यस्य॑ ते स॒प्त सिंध॑वः ।  
> अ॒नु॒क्षरं॑ति का॒कुदं॑ सू॒र्म्यं॑ सुषि॒रामि॑व ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सुऽदेवः । असि । वरुण । यस्य । ते । सप्त । सिंधवः ।  
> अनुऽक्षरंति । काकुदम् । सूर्म्यम् । सुषिराम्ऽइव ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वरुण जलाभिमानिन् देव त्वं सुदेवोऽसि यस्य सुदेवस्य ते तव काकुदं तालु समुद्राख्यं सप्त सिंधवः गंगाद्याः सप्त नद्यः अनुक्षरंति जिह्वायां सर्वदा स्रवंति ॥
> षोडशिशस्त्रे ‘यो व्यतीन् ' इति तृतीय आनुष्टुभस्तृचः । सूत्रितं च -- यो व्यतीरफाणयदिति तृचा आनुष्टुभाः' (आश्व. श्रौ. ६. २) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Varuṇa, god who presides over the waters, you are a good god; of you, such a good god, the palate, called the ocean, the seven rivers, Gaṅgā and the others, flow along, that is, they flow always on the tongue.

In the Śastra of the Ṣoḍaśin, the Ṛk beginning यो व्यतीन् (*yo vyatīn*) is the third in the Anuṣṭubh triplet. It is so laid down in the sūtra यो व्यतीरफाणयदिति तृचा आनुष्टुभाः (*yo vyatīraphāṇayaditi tṛcā ānuṣṭubhāḥ*) (Āśva. Śrau. 6. 2).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O god Varuṇa
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of such greatness
- ते (*te*)—your
- काकुदं (*kākudaṃ*)—to the root of the tongue
- सूर्म्यं (*sūrmyaṃ*)—the stream
- सुषिरां इव (*suṣirāṃ iva*)—as it falls into a pit that is like the nether world
- सप्त सिंधवः (*sapta siṃdhavaḥ*)—the seven rivers, Gaṅgā and the others
- अनुक्षरंति (*anukṣaraṃti*)—flow along; such as you are
- सुदेवः असि (*sudevaḥ asi*)—you are a most excellent god

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O god Varuṇa, you are a most excellent god, for the seven rivers, Gaṅgā and the others, flow to the root of your tongue, as a stream falls into a pit that is like the nether world.

##### English translation
You are a glorious god, Varuna, across whose palate the seven rivers keep pouring as a fair-flowing (stream) into an abyss.

### Printed p. 37

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

To explain the meaning of the word काकुदं (*kākudaṃ*), Yāska has given an explanation (nirvacana) for this Ṛk:
सुदेवस्त्वं कल्याणदेवः कमनीयदेवो वा भवसि वरुण यस्य ते सप्त सिंधवः । सिंधुः प्रवणात् यस्य ते सप्त स्रोतांसि तानि ते काकुदमनुस्यक्ष-रंति सूर्मिः कल्याणोर्मिः स्रोतः सुषिरमनु यथा ॥ (*sudevastvaṃ kalyāṇadevaḥ kamanīyadevo vā bhavasi varuṇa yasya te sapta siṃdhavaḥ | siṃdhuḥ pravaṇāt yasya te sapta srotāṃsi tāni te kākudamanusyakṣa-raṃti sūrmiḥ kalyāṇormiḥ srotaḥ suṣiramanu yathā ‖*) (Ni. 5-27) **NOTE**
O Varuṇa, you are Sudeva, that is, a god who brings about welfare, a god who does good to all, who makes auspiciousness; or else a god who is desired by all (a charming god). Such is your tongue's root, into which the seven famous rivers (Gaṅgā and the others) flow and fall. How? As flowing water goes and falls naturally into a pit, that is, into a low region, so the seven famous rivers go and fall into the root of your tongue (that is, into the ocean). Here the word Sindhu means a flowing river; that is, because it has the nature of flowing, a river is also called Sindhu. Sūrmi means one with good waves, that is, a stream with abundant water, as it rushes into a low region; thus the illustration of the word Suṣirā is given.

#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यो व्यतीँ॒रफा॑णय॒त्सुयु॑क्ताँ॒ उप॑ दा॒शुषे॑ ।  
> त॒क्वो ने॒ता तदिद्वपु॑रुप॒मा यो अमु॑च्यत ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । व्यतीन् । अफाणयत् । सुऽयुक्तान् । उप । दाशुषे ।  
> तक्वः । नेता । तत् । इत् । वपुः । उपऽमा । यः । अमुच्यत ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यः इंद्रः व्यतीन् विविधगमनान् ‘सुयुक्तान् सुष्ठु रथे संबद्धानश्वान् दाशुषे हविर्दात्रे यजमानाय गंतुं प्राप्तुम् उप अफाणयत् उपगमयति । फणतिर्गतिकर्मा । यदैवं करोति तदित् तदानीमेव तक्वः । तकतिर्गतिकर्मा । यज्ञगमनशीलः नेता उदकस्य फलस्य वा नायक इंद्रः वपुः उदकमुत्पादयतीति शेषः । यः इंद्रः उपमा उपमानभूतः अमुच्यत अन्यैर्वृष्टिनिवारकैरसुरादिभिर्मुक्तो भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, who causes his horses, of varied gait and well yoked, that is, well fastened to the chariot, to come near to the sacrificer who gives the oblation, to go to him and reach him (the root for "cause to go" is a root of motion): when he does so, at that very moment Indra, who is bent on going to the sacrifice (the root of the word for "swift" is a root of motion), the leader, who leads the water or the fruit, produces water; this is to be supplied. That Indra who is without compare, that is, serves as the standard of comparison, is freed from the others, the Asuras and the rest who hold back the rain.

### Printed p. 38

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who
- व्यतीन् (*vyatīn*)—those of many kinds of movement
- सुयुक्तान् (*suyuktān*)—his horses, well fastened to his chariot
- दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)—to come to the sacrificer who offers the oblation
